dev/log/issues/125/pulls/126/ci-logs/build-release-34455018919-jobs/93_Apply Changesets.txt:276:2026-09-10T08:25:04.7201200Z [main bc3cc33] 2.10.0: Make every CI/CD check answer from the data it actually has (issue #123). The nine runs on `main` at `1d9fb3e` carry **804** lines matching `warn` or `error` and exactly **7** annotations; three of the seven said something untrue. **Eight of the nine runs were green**, and six of the twenty root causes are in those eight. One sentence covers all of them: a check that reported a verdict about data it never obtained. ### The red run was two false verdicts stacked `Measure Disk Space and Update README` annotated `…did not finish within its 2400s budget and was terminated` — and then ran **19 m 21 s** more, until `timeout-minutes: 60` cancelled it. `kill -0` exits 1 for both ESRCH ("gone") and EPERM ("alive, not yours to signal"), and the survivors were root-owned `apt-get` children of a `sudo`, so `run-with-budget-warning.sh` read them as finished, skipped its SIGKILL escalation, and exited 124 believing it had terminated the command. They also held the step's stdout, which is why the step could not end. Liveness now comes from the process table, escalation runs SIGTERM → SIGKILL → SIGKILL under `sudo`, any survivor is named in an `::error`, and the wrapper relays output instead of sharing a pipe. The gate then excused the cancellation as a supersede: `check-pipeline-status.sh` asked one question of the **run** and let the answer excuse every cancelled **job** in it, and `measure-disk-space.yml` declares `cancel-in-progress: false`, which a supersede cannot reach at all. `read-job-cancel-in-progress.sh` reads the effective value out of the workflow file `GITHUB_WORKFLOW_REF` names, and everything unreadable fails closed. Either verdict alone would have left a red run; together they produced a green one. ### The checks that could not fail zizmor printed `WARN audit: zizmor is running in offline mode` and `no findings to report` in the same green run: both passes were `docker run` without `-e GH_TOKEN`, so every online audit was skipped — `known-vulnerable-actions` among them, the one audit that can catch a supply-chain compromise of an action already trusted here. Measured over a `tj-actions/changed-files@v44` fixture: offline 7 findings, online 8, and the 2 `known-vulnerable-actions` hits appear only online. `run-zizmor.sh` pins image, floors and targets, passes the token by name and treats the offline banner as a failure. `if-no-files-found: warn` reported the absence of the only record of a run as a line in the log of the run whose record is missing; both uploads now ask `steps.<id>.outcome` and require files. `brew link … 2>&1 | grep -v Warning || true` tested grep's opinion of the text at four sites, hiding a failure that says why, a failure that says only `Warning:`, and issue #53's `timeout` 124. `npm --force` rode on the Playwright install since issue #84; twelve runs across both images, fresh and re-install, before and after `npm install -g npm@latest`, all exit 0 with the same version — the flag is gone. ### Text that was read as a verdict, and a log that was erased The runner's `ActionCommand.TryParse` accepts `##[` anywhere in a physical line, so `git commit` echoing a changeset that quotes `##[error]` becomes a `failure` annotation on a successful job. `run-with-commands-stopped.sh` brackets a child's output with `::stop-commands::<fresh 128-bit token>` and always resumes, at 15 call sites. Sweeping for those printers found `cmd 2>&1 | tee /dev/stderr`: `/dev/stderr` is `/proc/self/fd/2`, so `tee` **reopens** the file behind fd 2 with `O_TRUNC` — harmless when fd 2 is a pipe, destructive when it is a file. `capture-and-stream.sh` streams through a duplicate of the descriptor instead. ### `git diff` exiting 128 is not "nothing changed" Three release gates ran `git diff --name-only "origin/$BASE...HEAD"` and discarded the status: with no `fetch-depth: 0`, a renamed base or a failed fetch, the range does not resolve, the command prints nothing, and two of the three gates report the passing verdict on a branch that rewrote `VERSION` with no changeset. `scripts/release/pr-diff-range.sh` is the one place that asks what a pull request changed; it restores a base ref missing only locally and otherwise names the cause on stderr and returns 1. Extracting it surfaced that the changeset gate's path set omitted `VERSION`, `.github/actions/` and `.githooks/`, and echoed pull-request-controlled paths with command processing live. Two `gh` reads conflated "empty" with "failed"; the remaining **61** statements where a git or `gh` read could be mistaken for an answer — **27** in `scripts/`, `.github/` and `.githooks/` — are dispositioned one by one in `analysis/git-read-failure-sweep.md`, with the corrected query that finds them and the reason the first one could not. ### "Nothing to check" is not the same as "could not look" Eight `scripts/ci` gates discover their own inputs with `git ls-files`. Five ended that listing in `|| true` and the other three built it inside a process substitution; either way a git that could not answer — a busy index, an unreadable object, no `git` on `PATH` — arrived as an empty list, and the gate reported the empty list as a fact about the repository. Measured against the shipped `run-shellcheck.sh` with a `git` exiting 128: `==> No shell scripts to check`, status **0**, over 201 tracked shell scripts it never opened. All eight now separate "git could not answer" from "git answered, and there is nothing", and exit 2 naming which — as does `run-precommit-checks.sh`, the hook driver that runs them, which read the **index** the same way and printed `Nothing staged; no checks to run` over a commit it had not seen. The release path asked the same question of `VERSION`: 18 hand-rolled reads across six workflows, 16 of them behind `git pull origin main || true`, where an unreadable file leaves `version=` — a tag published as `ghcr.io/…/box-js:-amd64`, or a bump to `1.0.0` from whatever the version actually was. `scripts/release/release-version.sh` is the single reader now, it takes the version the pipeline already resolved (`fromJSON(inputs.changes)['version']`), and it refuses an empty one. The last one is a break this branch committed itself: three orphan `echo` lines left `release-full.yml` unparseable (`Psych::SyntaxError … line 175 column 33`), and all four gates that read workflow files exited 0 while describing its contents — each reads YAML with a regex or a `grep`, so a file that is not YAML at all is simply one they extract nothing from. `scripts/ci/check-workflow-yaml.sh` parses every workflow with a real parser before the others run. One step further out is the same question about a gate's *subject* rather than its input: `validate-changeset.sh` matched `.changeset/` at any depth, so this branch's own release run failed with `Invalid changeset format` over `dev/log/…/templates/go/.changeset/add-changeset-workflow.md` — a changeset belonging to another repository, pinned here as the evidence the issue asks for, written in the changesets format rather than this repository's `bump:` one. True about the file, false about this repository: `apply-changesets.sh` reads `find .changeset -maxdepth 1` and would never have applied it. The pattern is anchored at the root now, at the consumer's depth, and every gate that discovers its own inputs was re-measured against an evidence tree that holds seven other projects' source — 1355 inputs across ten gates, none of them under `dev/log/`. ### The shell a runner hands you is not the shell you tested in `tr: write error: Broken pipe` and `grep: write error: Broken pipe` on two passing jobs: a step's shell starts with SIGPIPE `SIG_IGN` ([actions/runner#2684](https://github.com/actions/runner/issues/2684), open since 2023), an ignored disposition survives `exec`, and bash cannot reset one — so `producer | head` gets EPIPE, a stderr line, and under `pipefail` a non-zero pipeline. Both sites bound the **reader** so the writer reaches EOF. The same runner difference is why `useradd -m` printed `the home directory /home/box already exists. Not copying any file from skel`: `WORKDIR /home/box` stands above it and Docker creates the directory, so five images shipped a `box` user with no `~/.profile` — the file that sources `~/.bashrc` for a login shell — and `ssh`, `su - box` and `bash -l` saw none of the PATH the install scripts append. ### Six defects of this issue's own class, in the instruments built to measure it Three of the twenty root causes are in code this branch wrote. A sweep matched its own fixture text; a measurement was read before the data was in; a comparison read `/tmp/roles-*.txt`, a glob that matched its own other output. Three more were found only by the GitHub runner, on suites green on every machine here: an assertion that apt's default retry count *equals* 3 (it is 3 here and in `ubuntu:24.04`, **1** on `ubuntu-24.04`, same apt 2.8.3 on the same Ubuntu 24.04.4 — and the invariant this repository depends on is that `-o Acquire::Retries=3` is never a *downgrade*, which is a direction, not an equality); a "SIGPIPE at its default" leg that inherited the runner's `SIG_IGN` instead of establishing anything, making both legs the same leg; and a fake `ps` whose unkillable survivor died with the process group it was standing in for. All six are fixed at the root — a premise read out of `/proc/self/status`, a fixture that outlives what it stands in for, a private `mktemp -d` — never by loosening an assertion. ### Evidence, tests and what went upstream `dev/log/issues/123/pulls/124/` holds 7.4 MB of it: all nine run logs and all 99 jobs of the release run, the 804-line census with every line classified, all 7 annotations, the zizmor/apt/useradd/npm probes, a nine-document analysis, and the full role matrix of this repository's CI tree against all seven `link-foundation/*-ai-driven-development-pipeline-template` repositories, file by file, with 91 gaps dispositioned individually. Six defect classes found in that comparison were filed upstream as **19 issues and 1 comment** across six template repositories, each with a reproduction that runs offline, a workaround and a fix in diff form. Fifteen new offline suites, **551 assertions, 0 failures**, each checker exercised in a passing *and* a failing form and every sweep pinned to a site count. `PR_DIFF_RANGE_VERBOSE=1` (or `BOX_VERBOSE=1`) traces how a pull request's diff range was computed — default off, on stderr — and the apt suite prints its measured default, `apt-config dump`, every apt.conf line setting retries with its contents and whether `apt-get` is a wrapper, and fails if the dumped value and the measured default disagree, so the one question the evidence cannot answer today arrives explained on the next run. `docs/case-studies/issue-123/CASE-STUDY.md` has the whole analysis.
